As an application, we show that the realization space of the 24-cell has the expected local dimension at its symmetric regular realization, completing the local dimension program for regular 4-polytopes initiated by Rastanawi, Sinn, and Ziegler.

Smooth Counterexamples to the Eisenbud--Schreyer--Weyman Ulrich Existence Problem

Cristian Anghel

Do all projective algebraic varieties carry an Ulrich sheaf—a special vector bundle whose existence has deep implications for determinantal representations and complexity theory? This paper constructs smooth surface counterexamples showing the answer is no, disproving a 2003 conjecture of Eisenbud, Schreyer, and Weyman. The construction extends a numerical obstruction proposed by Beauville beyond the previously assumed Picard rank-one setting, revealing the obstruction is far more general.

math.AG math.AC
Original abstract

In their 2003 article in the Journal of the American Mathematical Society, Eisenbud, Schreyer and Weyman asked whether every embedded projective variety carries an Ulrich sheaf. In 2017 Beauville proposed a numerical route toward a surface with no Ulrich bundles: in Picard rank one, existence forces $H^2\ge K_S^2-8χ(\mathcal O_S)$, suggesting a search near the Bogomolov--Miyaoka--Yau boundary. We show that the Picard-rank-one hypothesis is not needed for the obstruction: a rank-independent Bogomolov--Hodge argument gives the same inequality on every smooth polarized surface. Using additional Neron--Severi directions on Hirzebruch--Kummer resolutions, the exponent-$3$ Hesse surface admits a very ample class $H=4A-E$ with $H^2=7\cdot3^9<16\cdot3^9=K_Y^2-8χ(\mathcal O_Y)$, hence a smooth counterexample to the Eisenbud--Schreyer--Weyman problem in its standard formulation. Consequently its Chow form has no ESW-type linear determinantal representation arising from an Ulrich sheaf on the embedded surface. Moreover, for every $n\ge3$ the Hesse pair $(Y_n,4A_n-E_n)$ is a counterexample, the surfaces are pairwise non-isomorphic, and $H_n^2/σ(Y_n)=7/(3n^2-11)\to0$, while $K_{Y_n}^2/χ(\mathcal O_{Y_n})\to60/7\approx8.5714$. A general arrangement-theoretic Rees-algebra/Segre mechanism yields further infinite families.

Non-nef loci in mixed characteristic

Ryotaro Iwane

In algebraic geometry, the 'non-nef locus' and 'restricted base locus' of a line bundle measure where positivity fails, and a conjecture of Boucksom–Broustet–Pacienza says these two notions always agree. This paper proves that conjecture in mixed characteristic — geometry that simultaneously involves both zero and prime characteristic, like schemes over the integers — for a broad class of mildly singular spaces. This fills a significant gap between known results in pure characteristic zero and positive characteristic settings.

math.AG
Original abstract

We introduce relative versions of non-nef loci and restricted base loci, and investigate a relative version of Boucksom--Broustet--Pacienza's conjecture that these two loci coincide. We prove the conjecture holds for normal integral schemes projective and flat over a Dedekind domain of characteristic zero with mild singularities, such as BCM-regularity. This result is a mixed characteristic analogue of results of Cacciola--Di Biagio in characteristic zero and Sato in positive characteristic. To prove this result, we show that BCM-regularity is preserved under perturbations by divisors with small vanishing order.

Flexibility of affine cones over blow-ups of weighted projective planes

In-Kyun Kim, Dae-Won Lee, Masatomo Sawahara

An 'affine cone' over a projective surface is a higher-dimensional algebraic variety constructed from it, and 'flexibility' means its symmetry group acts transitively outside a small set — intuitively, the variety has enormous algebraic freedom. This paper proves flexibility and near-flexibility of affine cones over blow-ups of weighted projective planes (surfaces obtained by replacing finitely many points of a projective plane with small curves), covering a wide range of cases. This extends known flexibility results to a new family of singular surfaces.

math.AG
Original abstract

Let $S_m^n$ be the surface obtained by blowing up $\mathbb{P}(1,1,m)$ at $n$ general smooth points, where $m\geq2$. For every very ample Cartier divisor $H$ on $S_m^n$, we prove that $\mathrm{Affcone}_H(S_m^n)$ is flexible when $n\leq m+1$. For $n=m+2$ and $n=m+3$, we prove that $\mathrm{Affcone}_H(S_m^n)$ is generically flexible.

Finite Directional Coefficient Modules over Euler Fibers of $A$-Hypergeometric Systems

Go Okuyama

A-hypergeometric systems are differential equations attached to integer matrices, whose solutions generalize classical hypergeometric functions. This paper characterizes the formal logarithmic solutions near singular points called Euler fibers, working without restrictive assumptions typically required in the field. The key result is that, for a generic direction, the equations determining solution coefficients reduce to a finite, tractable system — making these previously intractable solutions explicitly computable.

math.AG
Original abstract

Let \(A\) be a full-rank integer matrix, let \(β\) be a complex parameter, and consider the associated \(A\)-hypergeometric system. Without assuming homogeneity, pointedness, or positivity, we study directed formal logarithmic solutions over all exponent lattices in the Euler fiber. For a fixed lattice-generic direction, negative-support strata are described by rational sign polyhedra, and normalization reduces the coefficient equations on each exponent lattice to a finite directional coefficient module. We prove that only finitely many exponent lattices contribute nonzero directed solution spaces and that all contributing coefficient modules have finite length. Macaulay inverse-system duality identifies the dimension of the all-lattice solution space with the length of the corresponding all-lattice module. We also give an explicit formal-series realization and a polynomial colon-ideal criterion for all realizable lowest exponents.

Degree of irrationality of abelian surfaces

Songsong Huang, Zhi Jiang, Xin Yang

How hard is it to map an abelian surface (a two-dimensional complex torus with group structure) onto ordinary projective space by a rational map? The answer depends on the surface's polarization type—a measure of its geometric structure. This paper proves the minimum possible degree of such a dominant map is exactly 3 for four specific polarization types and 4 for all others, completely resolving the 'degree of irrationality' for abelian surfaces.

math.AG
Original abstract

Let $A$ be a complex abelian surface. We prove that $A$ admits a dominant rational map of degree three to $\PP^2$ if and only if it admits a polarization of type $(1,1)$, $(1,2)$, $(1,3)$, or $(1,6)$. Consequently, the degree of irrationality of $A$ is $3$ precisely in these four cases and is $4$ otherwise.

Contact fundamental forms and adjoint varieties

Baohua Fu, Jun-Muk Hwang

Classical projective differential geometry uses 'fundamental forms' to encode how a submanifold curves inside projective space; this paper builds a contact-geometry analogue. By replacing polynomial algebras with algebras tied to the Heisenberg group, the authors define contact fundamental forms and prove they satisfy a natural algebraic structure at generic points, extending a foundational theorem of Élie Cartan to the contact setting.

math.AG math.RT
Original abstract

We introduce contact symbol systems, a noncommutative analogue of symbol systems for projective fundamental forms, by replacing the polynomial algebra on a vector space by the graded dual of the universal enveloping algebra of a Heisenberg algebra. For a complex projective submanifold equipped with a contact structure, we define contact fundamental forms and prove that, at a general point, they form a contact symbol system, which gives a contact version of the classical result due to E. Cartan. Conversely, we prove that every contact symbol system can be realized as the contact fundamental forms of a projective variety with a dense open Heisenberg orbit, called the Heisenberg-symmetric variety associated to the contact symbol system. We show that the closure of a projectivized nilpotent orbit in a simple Lie algebra is Heisenberg-symmetric if and only if it is the adjoint variety, namely, the projectivization of the minimal nilpotent orbit. For adjoint varieties of non-symplectic simple Lie algebras, we prove the contact analogue of the Landsberg--Manivel strict prolongation property by using Yamaguchi's prolongation theory.

A logarithmic Bogomolov--Sommese vanishing theorem on compact Kähler manifolds

Zhi Li, Xiankui Meng, Kai Pang et al.

Vanishing theorems tell you when certain cohomology groups—algebraic invariants of geometric spaces—must be zero, imposing strong constraints on a manifold's geometry. This paper proves a logarithmic Bogomolov–Sommese vanishing theorem on compact Kähler manifolds for pseudo-effective line bundles, then applies it to show that logarithmic cotangent bundles with vanishing second Chern class are rigid, settling a natural question in complex geometry.

math.AG math.CV
Original abstract

In this paper, we establish a logarithmic Bogomolov--Sommese vanishing theorem in terms of numerical dimension for pseudo-effective line bundles on compact Kähler manifolds. As an application, we obtain a rigidity result with vanishing second Chern class for logarithmic cotangent bundles by combining the vanishing theorem with a structure theorem of Iwai and Matsumura.

Essential Dimension and Faithful Rank of Finite p-Gerbes

Tianzhi Yang

Essential dimension measures how many independent parameters are needed to describe an algebraic object. This paper extends the Karpenko–Merkurjev theorem—which links essential dimension to representation theory for finite p-groups—to the broader setting of finite p-gerbes (twisted, stacky versions of group schemes), proving that essential dimension at a prime p equals the minimum faithful rank achievable after a prime-to-p field extension.

math.AG
Original abstract

Let $p\neq\operatorname{char}(k)$. We extend the Karpenko--Merkurjev theorem from classifying stacks of finite $p$-groups to arbitrary finite gerbes whose geometric inertia groups are $p$-groups, without assuming that the gerbe is neutral or that its band is represented by a group scheme over the base field. We prove that the essential dimension at $p$ is exactly the minimum faithful rank obtained after prime-to-$p$ base change, equivalently the faithful rank over a $p$-closure. We also prove a relative form of the theorem for locally full morphisms of finite $p$-gerbes: the relative faithful rank equals the supremum of the essential $p$-dimensions of the fibers. Finally, we introduce the quotient compression dimension, defined using tame quotient singularities with prescribed fundamental gerbe. For every finite $p$-gerbe $\mathcal{G}/k$ we show that its prime local version satisfies $$ \mathrm{ed}_k(\mathcal{G};p) \leq \operatorname{qcdim}_p(\mathcal{G}) \leq \mathrm{ed}_k(\mathcal{G};p)+1. $$ Thus essential dimension at $p$ determines, up to at most one dimension, the smallest quotient singularity realizing the gerbe after prime-to-$p$ localization.

A linear bound for Fujita's freeness conjecture

Jingjun Han

Fujita's freeness conjecture predicts that for a smooth n-dimensional algebraic variety with an ample divisor L, the line bundle K_X + mL becomes globally generated (has enough sections to embed the variety) once m ≥ n+1. This paper proves a linear bound m ≥ 1.777n works unconditionally, improving prior results via a sharp new estimate on singularity multiplicity. It's the strongest general progress toward Fujita's conjecture to date.

math.AG
Original abstract

Let $X$ be a smooth complex projective variety of dimension $n$, and let $L$ be an ample Cartier divisor. We prove that $K_X+mL$ is globally generated for every integer $m\geq\lceil C_0n\rceil$, where $C_0=1.77629\ldots$ is an explicit constant. In particular, $K_X+2nL$ is globally generated. Our main input is a new estimate for the multiplicity of a minimal log canonical center. If $(X,Δ)$ is log canonical near a closed point $x$ but is not klt at $x$, and $W$ is the positive-dimensional minimal log canonical center through $x$, then $2\overline{e}_1(\mathfrak m_{W,x})\leq\bigl(\dim W-\operatorname{lct}_x((X,Δ);\mathfrak m_x)\bigr)\operatorname{mult}_xW$, where $\overline{e}_1(\mathfrak m_{W,x})$ is the first normal Hilbert coefficient of the maximal ideal of $\mathcal O_{W,x}$. This implies $\operatorname{mult}_xW\leq \frac{(a+c)^{a+c}}{a^ac^c}$, where $a:=\frac{\dim W-\operatorname{lct}_x((X,Δ);\mathfrak m_{x})}{2}$ and $c:=\operatorname{edim}\mathcal O_{W,x}-\dim W$.

144 real circles tangent to three conics

Taylor Brysiewicz

How many circles can be simultaneously tangent to three given conics? The long-standing conjecture said at most 136. This paper constructs an explicit configuration of three conics with 144 such tritangent circles, directly disproving the conjectured bound and reopening the classical enumerative geometry question.

math.AG
Original abstract

We exhibit three conics with 144 tritangent circles, disproving the conjectured maximum of 136.

K-stability and birational rigidity of Fano 3-fold weighted complete intersections

Takuzo Okada

A Fano variety is a shape with positive curvature, and K-stability is an algebraic condition that guarantees the existence of a nice 'Kähler-Einstein' metric on it. Birational rigidity means the variety has essentially no other rational structure — it cannot be simplified or related to other shapes by birational maps. This paper proves that every birationally rigid well-formed quasismooth Fano 3-fold weighted complete intersection is K-stable, connecting two previously separate notions of geometric rigidity.

math.AG
Original abstract

We show that any birationally rigid well-formed and quasismooth Fano 3-fold weighted complete intersection is K-stable.

Virasoro Constraints for Orbifold Curves

Jianxun Hu, Pengrong Jin, Hua-Zhong Ke et al.

Virasoro constraints are a recursive system of equations governing Gromov–Witten invariants, which count curves inside a geometric space. This paper proves these constraints for orbifold curves — spaces that locally look like quotients by finite group actions — including a previously conjectural 'absolute' version due to Jiang–Tseng. The result completes a significant piece of the enumerative geometry program for orbifold targets.

math.AG math-ph
Original abstract

We prove the Virasoro constraints for the relative Gromov--Witten theory of all smooth projective effective orbifold curves, allowing relative conditions at ordinary points. The absolute Jiang--Tseng Virasoro conjecture for smooth projective effective orbifold curves follows as a corollary.

Openness of Fourier--Mukai loci for algebraic spaces

Elías Guisado Villalgordo, Pat Lank

A Fourier–Mukai transform is a powerful tool in algebraic geometry that moves objects between two spaces via a shared 'kernel'; being fully faithful or an equivalence are key quality conditions. This paper proves that the set of base-points where these conditions hold is open — a fundamental continuity result — extended to the general setting of algebraic spaces. This resolves a technical gap needed for moduli-theoretic applications.

math.AG math.AC
Original abstract

We develop a theory of relative perfect complexes on algebraic spaces. Our main result establishes openness for the loci where fibers of an integral transform are fully faithful (resp.\ an equivalence).

Towards Categorical Kähler Geometry

Fabian Haiden, Ludmil Katzarkov, Maxim Kontsevich et al.

Kähler geometry studies smooth spaces equipped with rich geometric structure via a special metric. This paper sketches a version of that theory for 'derived noncommutative geometry'—a highly abstract setting where spaces are replaced by algebraic categories. The authors propose analogues of key Kähler concepts (harmonic metrics, energy-minimizing flows, curvature potentials) in this new context, refining Bridgeland stability conditions, a central tool in modern algebraic geometry.

math.AG math.RT math.SG
Original abstract

We outline the contours of an emerging theory of Kähler metrics in derived noncommutative geometry. This is a refinement of the theory of Bridgeland stability conditions encoding underlying differential-geometric structures. We propose elements of such a structure in both Archimedean and non-Archimedean settings, including metrized objects, mass measures satisfying a BPS inequality, harmonic metrics, minimizing flows, and complexified Kähler potentials. We develop the framework through examples and constructions involving Fukaya categories, quiver representations and associated C$^*$-algebras, spectral networks, and comonadic adjunctions of stable $\infty$-categories.

Extended Abstract of Counting Divisorial Contractions with Centre a $cA_n$-Singularity

Erik Paemurru

In 3D algebraic geometry, a divisorial contraction near a cA_n singularity (a mild, classifiable type of singular point) shrinks a surface to a curve or point. This extended abstract reviews results counting how many essentially distinct such contractions exist. The classification matters because these contractions are building blocks in the Minimal Model Program, the main framework for understanding the structure of higher-dimensional algebraic varieties.

math.AG
Original abstract

This text is chapter 149 of the ZAG Handbook of Algebraic Geometry. We review the main results of the paper "Counting divisorial contractions with centre a $cA_n$-singularity", published in Publications of the Research Institute for Mathematical Sciences, Kyoto University.

An integral Hyodo--Kato isomorphism

Federico Binda, Shuji Saito

The Hyodo–Kato theorem connects two cohomology theories—log crystalline cohomology of a scheme's special fiber and de Rham cohomology of its generic fiber—but only after inverting primes, losing integral (torsion) information. This paper lifts that isomorphism to work integrally, answering a question posed by Fontaine and Jannsen, which matters for understanding p-adic Hodge theory with full arithmetic precision.

math.AG
Original abstract

Let $\mathscr{O}_K$ be a mixed characteristic complete DVR with perfect residue field $k$, and let $\mathfrak{X}$ be a proper formal scheme over $\mathscr{O}_K$ with semistable reduction. Answering a question of Fontaine and Jannsen, a classical theorem of Hyodo and Kato gives a rational identification between the log crystalline cohomology of the special fiber $\mathfrak{X}_0$ over $W(k)^0$ with the de Rham cohomology of the generic fiber $\mathfrak{X}_K$. In this note, we use a log variant of the saturated de Rham--Witt complex of Bhatt--Lurie--Mathew to prove that such a comparison holds integrally.

Infinite transitivity of tame groups of automorphisms of affine spaces

Alexander Borisov, Ofer Gabber, Adrian Vasiu

How efficiently can you move any set of m points in n-dimensional space over a finite field to any other configuration, using 'tame' polynomial maps (built from simple linear and elementary operations)? This paper proves the tame automorphism group acts infinitely transitively—such rearrangements always exist—and establishes tight bounds on the polynomial degree needed. This settles questions about the reach and complexity of structured symmetries of affine spaces over finite fields.

math.AG math.NT
Original abstract

For positive integers $n$ and $m$, we study the actions of the groups of tame automorphisms of the $n$-dimensional affine spaces over finite fields on ordered subsets of $m$ points. Our primary interest lies in constructing tame automorphisms that take one ordered sequence to another and in proving upper and lower bounds on the maximal complexity of such automorphisms. Our preferred measure of complexity of an automorphism is the maximum of the degrees of the polynomials that define it and its inverse. Using methods and results from various branches of mathematics, including the theory of symmetric groups, affine geometry over finite fields, polynomial interpolation, combinatorics of projective spaces over fields, and polynomial automorphisms, we obtain a wide variety of qualitative and quantitative results.

Weighted Syzygies of Pointed Curves

Maya Banks, John Cobb, Mahrud Sayrafi

When a curve is embedded in projective space, its defining equations and their relations (syzygies) encode deep geometric information — but what happens for non-standard embeddings? This paper studies the algebraic structure of curves embedded via low-degree linear series attached to a single point, bounding the degrees of generators and their syzygies. The results extend Green's classical Np properties to this weighted setting, clarifying how geometry controls algebra even when standard generation assumptions fail.

math.AG math.AC
Original abstract

For a point $P$ on a smooth projective curve $C$ of genus $g$, the section ring $R_d = R(C,\mathcal{O}_C(dP))$ can be minimally presented as a quotient $S_d/I_d$ where $S_d$ is a $\mathbb{Z}$-graded polynomial ring. Motivated by Green's $N_p$ properties for projective embeddings, we investigate the syzygies of $R_d$ over $S_d$ in low degrees $d$ when $R_d$ is not generated in degree 1. We bound the degrees of the generators of $R_d$ and prove uniform column-by-column bounds on the support of the Betti table of $R_d$ over $S_d$. We compute the weighted regularity of $R_d$ and show that if $d$ is larger than the Frobenius number of $P$ then $R_d$ satisfies the weighted $N_p$ condition, where $p=g-1-\binom{d-g}{2}$. Finally, we give sufficient criteria for the Betti numbers to be determined explicitly and show that for ordinary points, the resolution of $R_{g+1}$ is pure.

The Absolute Twistor Line and the Geometry of $\overline{\text{Spec}\, \mathbf Z}$

Alain Connes, Caterina Consani

Can the integers Spec ℤ — the fundamental object underlying all of number theory — be compactified into a geometric space analogous to a curve? This paper constructs such a compactification using F₁ (the 'field with one element,' a theoretical object unifying arithmetic and geometry) and adjoins an imaginary unit to produce a 'twistor line' with a canonical symmetry. The result gives Spec ℤ a genuine geometric boundary, advancing the program of absolute algebraic geometry.

math.AG math.CT
Original abstract

We construct the absolute algebraic geometry of the compactification $\overline{\text{Spec}\, \mathbf Z}$ by amalgamating the affine absolute curve $(\text{Spec}\, \mathbf Z)_{\mathbf{F}_{1}}$ with an archimedean component defined over the signed extension $\mathbf{F}_{1^2}$ of $\mathbf{F}_1$. By adjoining a formal imaginary unit to the absolute projective line, we obtain an equivariant topos endowed with a canonical geometric inversion symmetry, which induces the twistor real structure on its complex points. This archimedean geometry is incorporated into a global absolute curve defined as an internal object of the odd arithmetic topos, dual to the multiplicative monoid of odd positive integers, and governed by the intrinsic Hopf structure of spherical $\mathbf{F}_{1^2}$-algebras. The restriction of the absolute Frobenius action to odd integers is forced arithmetically by the extension of scalars to $\mathbf{F}_{1^2}$. On complex points, the resulting dynamics simultaneously generates the Adams operations and complex conjugation on real Hodge structures. At the categorical level, the odd arithmetic topos originates in the pericyclic category, whose $λ$-operations provide a conceptual interpretation of the local factors of geometric L-functions.

Nonexistence of degree two rational multisections of conic bundles over the plane

Jeffrey Diller, Lena Ji, Eric Riedl

A conic bundle is a geometric object fibered by conics (degree-2 curves); a rational multisection of degree two is a surface that intersects each fiber in exactly two points, defined by rational functions. This paper proves that sufficiently generic conic bundle threefolds over the plane have no such degree-two multisections, the first step toward Iskovskikh's conjecture that some conic bundles are not unirational — meaning they cannot be parametrized by rational functions — which would be a landmark result in algebraic geometry.

math.AG
Original abstract

We prove that a standard conic bundle $X \to \mathbb P^2_{\mathbb C}$ whose discriminant is very general of degree at least 18 admits no rational multisections of degree two. This is the first step towards proving a conjecture of Iskovskikh that there are conic bundle threefolds that are not unirational, since to prove that $X$ is not unirational, it suffices to show that there are no rational multisections of any degree. Proving Iskovskikh's conjecture would provide the first example of a rationally connected variety that is not unirational.

Subleading Collinear Limits of Yang-Mills Amplitudes from Gravity

Jin Dong, Stephan Stieberger

When two particles in a scattering amplitude become nearly parallel (collinear limit), the amplitude simplifies in a predictable way; subleading terms capture finer corrections to this behavior. This paper shows that these subleading collinear limits in Yang-Mills theory — describing gauge bosons — are fully controlled by Einstein-Yang-Mills amplitudes involving gravitons, revealing an unexpected gravity/gauge-theory connection. The results also yield new BCJ-like relations, reducing the complexity of computing these limits.

hep-th gr-qc hep-ph math.AG math.CO
Original abstract

We show that the subleading collinear sector of Yang-Mills (YM) amplitudes is controlled by ordinary Einstein-Yang-Mills (EYM) amplitudes and their higher-derivative corrections. The complete set of strict subleading collinear limits associated with an equal-helicity collinear pair can be extracted from open-closed string disk amplitudes. The latter generate $\frac12 (n-3)!$ BCJ-like relations with non-linear kinematic coefficients, reducing the $(n-3)!$ collinear data to a basis of dimension $\frac12 (n-3)!$. This reduced basis can be represented by gravitational amplitudes in EYM theories and by their higher-derivative open-string corrections. At multiplicity $n$ this gravitational basis consists of the $(n-4)!$ independent EYM subamplitudes together with $\frac12 (n-5)(n-4)!$ higher-order corrections involving one graviton and $n-2$ gluons. The resulting decomposition is governed by unsigned Stirling numbers of the first kind: the ordinary EYM amplitudes correspond to the sector $\left[{n-3\atop 1}\right]$, the higher-order BCJ-like relations to even sectors $\left[{n-3\atop 2j}\right]$, and the higher-derivative EYM corrections to odd sectors $\left[{n-3\atop 2j+1}\right]$ with $j\geq 1$.

Bosonic codes from compact phase spaces

David Roberts, Aaron Slipper, Alireza Parhizkar et al.

Bosonic quantum error-correcting codes protect quantum information from noise using infinite-dimensional systems. This paper constructs such codes on genus-two Riemann surfaces using automorphic forms, then proves a no-go theorem: for higher-genus surfaces, the stabilizer group is non-amenable (too large to average over), forcing a spectral gap that makes perfect quantum error correction impossible with normalizable states. This reveals fundamental topological obstructions in quantum coding theory.

quant-ph math-ph math.AG
Original abstract

We present the algebraic structure of bosonic quantum error-correcting codes on genus-two Riemann surfaces. We explicitly construct the code words as automorphic forms and analytically generate the full tower of code spaces at all weights. We prove a fundamental no-go theorem: for any genus greater than one, the stabilizer group is non-amenable, forcing a strictly positive spectral gap in the stabilizer Hamiltonian. Consequently, no normalizable quantum state can satisfy all stabilizer conditions. This sharply contrasts with standard Gottesman-Kitaev-Preskill (GKP) codes, where the amenability of the stabilizer group $\mathbb{Z}^2$ permits approximate code words with arbitrary precision.

Degenerations of elliptic quartics and K-moduli of Fano threefolds

Ivan Cheltsov, Anne-Sophie Kaloghiros, Robert Śmiech et al.

K-stability is an algebro-geometric condition that predicts when a Fano variety—a type of positively curved geometric space—admits a canonical metric. This paper studies a specific family of Fano threefolds built by two successive blow-ups of projective 3-space, proving their K-moduli space (which parametrizes K-stable members) is isomorphic to a classical geometric quotient. The result fully classifies which members of this family are K-stable, connecting modern moduli theory to concrete birational geometry.

math.AG
Original abstract

We study the K-moduli space of Fano threefolds obtained by first blowing up $\mathbb{P}^3$ along an elliptic quartic curve $C$ and then blowing up a fiber $\ell_p$ of the exceptional divisor $E \to C$. We prove that this K-moduli space is isomorphic to a VGIT quotient parametrizing pairs $(C,p)$, with linearization induced by the CM line bundle. In particular, we classify all K-(semi/poly)stable members of this deformation family. The main new ingredients in the proof include the geometry of the Hilbert scheme of elliptic quartic curves, deformation theory of Fano--K3 pairs, and optimal bounds on the local volumes of threefold singularities.

On the first-order deformations of seminormal affine toric varieties

Konstantin Egert, Hendrik Süß

First-order deformations measure how an algebraic variety can be 'wiggled' infinitesimally; for toric varieties—geometric objects built from combinatorial data called fans—these deformations are encoded in a module T^1. This paper gives an explicit description of T^1 for affine toric varieties that need not be normal (a standard regularity assumption), with a complete and concrete computation in the surface case. This fills a significant gap since most prior work assumed normality.

math.AG
Original abstract

We provide a general description of the module $T^1_X$ of first-order infinitesimal deformations of a not necessarily normal affine toric variety $X$ with a special emphasis on seminormal varieties satisfying Serre's $(S_2)$ condition. In the surface case we are able to give a very detailed and concrete picture which includes the dimension of each homogeneous component.

Abundance of affine algebraic structures on stabilized cotangent bundles of surfaces

Yin Li

A cotangent bundle is a natural complex manifold built from a surface, carrying rich geometric structure. This paper shows that for any surface of genus 2 or higher, the product of its cotangent bundle with the complex line admits uncountably many non-isomorphic smooth affine (algebraic) structures, forming a family parametrized by a space of dimension 6g−7. This resolves a question of Ivan Smith, revealing that algebraic structure on even 'standard' complex manifolds is far from unique.

math.AG math.CV math.SG
Original abstract

We show that for any $g\geq2$, there exist uncountably many non-isomorphic smooth affine threefolds that are Stein deformation equivalent to $T^\astΣ_g\times\mathbb{C}$, the product of the cotangent bundle of a genus $g$ oriented surface with the complex plane, answering a question of Ivan Smith. In fact, we prove that our family of smooth affine threefolds is parametrized by a $(6g-7)$-dimensional complex orbifold.

No Equivariant Architecture Covers All Equivariant Attention

Tīkun Ông

Equivariant neural networks are designed so their outputs transform predictably when inputs are symmetrically transformed — valuable for physics and geometry tasks. This paper fully characterizes which symmetry groups can be exactly realized by multi-head self-attention layers, showing that exact equivariance forces a restricted structure that inevitably limits expressiveness. No single fixed equivariant attention architecture can capture all equivariant attention maps, revealing a fundamental expressivity-symmetry tradeoff.

cs.LG math.AG math.RT
Original abstract

We give a complete characterization of equivariant multi-head self-attention (MHSA): if an MHSA layer is equivariant to a symmetry group $G$, then $G$ can only act by permuting head-clusters, with QK and OV matrices satisfying an equivariance constraint tied to the group action. As a consequence, we prove that any fixed MHSA architecture that achieves exact equivariance by polynomially parameterizing unconstrained MHSA parameters inevitably leads to expressivity loss within the class of equivariant maps: the equivariance locus of unconstrained MHSA forms a union of extremely many Zariski-irreducible components in a reduced parameter space, and any single architecture covers at most one. For $G=D_4$ acting on $C$ copies of the regular representation as the token feature space, we show that there are $Ω(C^{64})$ components for eight attention heads.

Tropical Matsusaka and Matsusaka--Ran Criteria

Zhijie Ji

The classical Schottky problem asks which abstract abelian varieties arise as Jacobians of curves; this paper tackles its tropical analogue, where geometry is replaced by combinatorial piecewise-linear objects. The authors prove tropical versions of two classical characterization theorems (Matsusaka and Matsusaka–Ran criteria), showing their conditions are equivalent, and together with prior work this yields a complete geometric solution to the tropical Schottky problem.

math.AG
Original abstract

The tropical Schottky problem asks which principally polarized tropical abelian varieties are isomorphic to tropical Jacobian varieties equipped with their canonical principal polarizations. We prove the tropical Matsusaka criterion and the tropical Matsusaka--Ran criterion, which are the main results of this paper, and show that their hypotheses are equivalent. Together with the tropical Poincaré formula proved by Gross--Shokrieh, these results give a geometric solution to the tropical Schottky problem. In particular, we give an affirmative answer to a question posed by Brannetti--Melo--Viviani. Several tools are developed for the proofs of the two criteria. In particular, we establish Hodge-type inequalities for ample tropical Cartier divisors on tropical abelian varieties and prove a splitting criterion for principally polarized tropical abelian varieties.

A New Algebraic Algorithm for LWE

Luca Campa, Massimo Fumiani, Arnab Roy

Learning With Errors (LWE)—where you must find a secret vector given noisy linear equations over integers—underpins most post-quantum cryptography. This paper introduces a new algebraic attack combining linear algebra with Gröbner basis S-polynomial techniques, offering a fresh algorithmic angle on Search-LWE and potentially informing how parameters should be chosen to keep cryptosystems secure.

cs.CR math.AG
Original abstract

The Learning With Errors (LWE) problem, introduced by Regev in 2005, is central to modern cryptography and post-quantum security. The algorithms to solve the search version of the problem, Search-LWE, can be broadly categorised into algebraic, combinatorial and lattice-based. In this work we propose a new algebraic algorithm for the Search-LWE problem. At a high level, the algorithm combines linear-algebraic techniques with S-polynomial-based methods from Groebner basis computation. We provide a direct complexity analysis of our algorithm, avoiding semi-regularity assumptions and complexity bounds derived from the degree of regularity. Our algorithm achieves a polynomial improvement in complexity over prior results that use Groebner basis methods to solve Search-LWE.

Birational Automorphism Bounds for General-Type Foliations on Surfaces via Pluricanonical Indices

Shi Xu

How large can the symmetry group of a foliation on a surface be? A foliation is a decomposition of a surface into curves; this paper bounds the size of finite groups of birational symmetries for foliations of general type using pluricanonical indices—algebraic invariants measuring how sections of powers of the canonical bundle grow. The results extend classical automorphism-bound theorems from algebraic geometry into the foliation setting.

math.AG
Original abstract

Let $\mathcal{F}$ be a canonical foliation of general type on a smooth projective surface $X$, and write $\mathrm{vol}(\mathcal{F}):=\mathrm{vol}(K_{\mathcal{F}})$. Let $G\subseteq\operatorname{Bir}(X,\mathcal{F})$ be a finite subgroup, and let $\mathcal{G}:=\mathcal{F}/G$ be the quotient foliation in the birational sense. For a canonical foliation $\mathcal{H}$, define its $r$-th pluricanonical index by \[ δ_r(\mathcal{H}) := \min \bigl\{ m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0} \mid h^0(mK_{\mathcal{H}})\geq r \bigr\}, \] where $\min\varnothing:=\infty$. For an arbitrary foliation, these indices are computed on any canonical birational model. If $κ(\mathcal{G})\geq0$, we prove \[ |G| \leq \begin{cases} 4δ_1(\mathcal{G})\,\mathrm{vol}(\mathcal{F}), &κ(\mathcal{G})=0,\\[1mm] \displaystyle \frac{4}{3}δ_2(\mathcal{G})\,\mathrm{vol}(\mathcal{F}), &κ(\mathcal{G})=1,\\[3mm] δ_2(\mathcal{G})^2 \bigl(1+δ_2(\mathcal{G})\bigr)\, \mathrm{vol}(\mathcal{F}), &κ(\mathcal{G})=2. \end{cases} \] Since $\operatorname{Bir}(X,\mathcal{F})$ is finite, one may in particular take $G=\operatorname{Bir}(X,\mathcal{F})$. When $κ(\mathcal{G})=0$ or $1$, the effective bounds $δ_1(\mathcal{G})\leq12$ and $δ_2(\mathcal{G})\leq42$ give \[ |G|\leq48\,\mathrm{vol}(\mathcal{F}) \qquad\text{and}\qquad |G|\leq56\,\mathrm{vol}(\mathcal{F}), \] respectively. The main new ingredient is a cluster formula for adjoint volumes, which yields index-dependent lower bounds for tangency-free foliated surface pairs whose underlying foliation has Kodaira dimension zero or one.

Wall-crossing formula and genus-one Virasoro conjecture for Fano complete intersections

Shuai Guo, Qingsheng Zhang, Yang Zhou

The Virasoro conjecture predicts an infinite family of constraints on Gromov-Witten invariants—numbers that count how many curves of a given shape fit inside an algebraic variety. It was previously verified only when the theory is 'semisimple,' a technical condition most varieties fail. This paper proves the genus-one case for Fano complete intersections (common geometric objects like hypersurfaces in projective space) where semisimplicity fails almost everywhere.

math.AG math-ph
Original abstract

The Virasoro conjecture predicts a set of universal relations among all genera Gromov--Witten invariants of any smooth projective variety. The conjecture is well understood for semisimple theories, but remains largely open in the non-semisimple setting. We prove the genus-one Virasoro conjecture on the ambient state space of smooth Fano complete intersections in projective space. For most of these complete intersections, the big quantum cohomology is nowhere semisimple. We also generalize the wall-crossing formula for quasimap invariants with weighted markings to the equivariant twisted setting, allowing descendant insertions at light markings. Together with genus-one quantum Lefschetz for quasimaps with light markings, this wall-crossing formula provides the key bridge from the Gromov--Witten theory of the complete intersection to the semisimple equivariant twisted theory of the projective space.

Weil's Theorem for Logarithmic Connections on Irreducible Nodal Curves

Sourav Das

Weil's classical theorem says a vector bundle on a smooth curve admits a connection exactly when its degree is zero; this paper extends that result to nodal curves — curves with simple self-intersection singularities — where 'connection' is replaced by the more subtle notion of a logarithmic connection relative to the dualizing sheaf. The degree-zero condition still governs existence, even in this singular setting, and the proof tracks how connections behave on the smooth normalization.

math.AG
Original abstract

We establish an analogue of André Weil's classical theorem for irreducible nodal curves. Let \(X_0\) be an irreducible projective nodal curve. We prove that an indecomposable vector bundle or torsion-free coherent sheaf \(E\) on \(X_0\) admits a holomorphic logarithmic connection \(\nabla\colon E\to E\otimesω_{X_0}\) with respect to the dualizing sheaf if and only if \(°E=0\). Moreover, when \(°E=0\), such a connection can be chosen so that the induced logarithmic connection on the normalization has scalar residues \(λ\cdot I\) at one preimage of the node and \(-λ\cdot I\) at the other, for some \(λ\in\mathbb{C}\). Explicit one-parameter families of flat connections are constructed on the irreducible rational nodal cubic curve as an illustration.

Infinitesimal Deformations of Generalized Parabolic Hitchin Pairs

Sourav Das

Hitchin pairs—vector bundles equipped with an extra 'Higgs field'—are central objects in geometry and mathematical physics. This paper develops deformation theory for a generalized parabolic version on singular nodal curves, identifying the space of first-order deformations via hypercohomology of an explicit complex. The payoff is proving the moduli space of such pairs is smooth of the expected dimension, a key structural result for these singular-curve analogues.

math.AG
Original abstract

We develop the infinitesimal deformation theory of generalized parabolic Hitchin pairs (GPHs) on irreducible nodal curves. For any GPH $(E,φ,F(E))$ we associate an explicit three-term deformation complex $\mathcal{C}_{(E_\bullet,φ)}$ whose first hypercohomology $\mathbb{H}^1(\mathcal{C}_{(E_\bullet,φ)})$ parameterizes first-order deformations. As the main application, we prove that the coarse moduli space $\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{GPH}}$ of $1$-stable generalized parabolic Hitchin pairs of rank $n$ and degree $d$ with $\gcd(n,d)=1$ is a local complete intersection, smooth in codimension one, and hence a normal variety. We further show that this moduli space carries a natural Poisson structure.

Generalized Hamming Weights of AJ-Gorenstein One-Point Codes

Eliseo Sarmiento-Rosales, José Alberto Guzmán-Vega, Juan Carlos Jiménez-Cervantes

Generalized Hamming weights measure how robustly a code's subcodes are spread across codeword positions, capturing a code's performance against adversarial erasure. This paper computes these weights for a class of algebraic-geometry codes built on AJ-Gorenstein curves by organizing them into a structured 'zero diagram,' then exploiting duality theorems to extract the full weight hierarchy. The approach yields explicit formulas and illuminates the geometric structure underlying the codes' error-correcting properties.

cs.IT math.AG
Original abstract

We study generalized Hamming weights along the one-point code flag of an AJ-Gorenstein curve. We organize these weights in a graded array, the zero diagram, whose entries are generalized coweights: the largest numbers of evaluation points on which subcodes of prescribed dimensions vanish simultaneously. Twisted and Wei duality show that each row of the zero diagram determines both the generalized Hamming weights of a lower block of short codes and the missing weights of a reflected upper block of long codes in the GHW diagram of the complete flag. Our main quantitative result is a uniform coverage theorem. For an AJ-Gorenstein curve of genus $g$ and evaluation length $n>2g$, the proportion of generalized-weight positions determined exactly throughout the complete flag satisfies $\operatorname{Cov}_{\mathrm{full}} \ge \frac{n(n-1)+4g}{n(n+2g-1)}>\frac12$. Thus more than half of all generalized-weight positions in the complete flag are determined uniformly. For the smallest Suzuki curve, the general and Castle-specific mechanisms together determine $2{,}280$ of the $2{,}912$ positions, giving an exact coverage of $78.30\%$.

The Picard number of fibred Mori dream surfaces

Federico Fallucca, Roberto Pignatelli, Francesco Polizzi

The Picard number of a surface counts the independent divisor classes in its geometry, but computing it can be hard. For surfaces with a fibration over a curve that are Mori dream spaces (surfaces with especially well-behaved cone of effective divisors), this paper shows the Picard number equals a simple count of irreducible components in the reducible fibers. This turns an abstract algebraic invariant into a concrete combinatorial calculation.

math.AG
Original abstract

Let $S$ be a smooth complex projective surface endowed with a fibration $f \colon S \to C$ onto a smooth projective curve $C$. We prove that, if $S$ is a Mori dream space (or, more generally, if its pseudo-effective cone is polyhedral) then the Picard number $ρ(S)$ can be effectively computed by counting the irreducible components of the reducible fibres of $f$. A first simple consequence is that, given an elliptic fibration $f \colon S \to \mathbb{P}^1$ with a section and such that $S$ is a Mori dream space, the Mordell-Weil group of the general fibre of $f$ is finite. The main application is a simple criterion for proving that a surface fibred over a curve is not a Mori dream space. We show that certain Horikawa surfaces, Fermat surfaces in $\mathbb{P}^{3}$ of every degree $\ge 4$, particular product-quotient surfaces, and the minimal simply connected numerical Godeaux surface constructed by Craighero and Gattazzo are not Mori dream spaces.

Analysis of PDEs

64

Smooth autonomous fast dynamo action on the three-torus

Michele Coti Zelati, Massimo Sorella, David Villringer

The fast dynamo conjecture, posed by Zeldovich and Sakharov, asks whether a smooth, steady fluid flow can exponentially amplify a magnetic field — a key question in magnetohydrodynamics. This paper resolves it affirmatively, constructing an explicit open family of smooth, time-independent, divergence-free vector fields on the three-torus that produce fast dynamo action via a stretch-fold-shear mechanism.

math.AP math.DS
Original abstract

We construct a nonempty $C^k$-open family, for some $k\in\mathbb{N}$, of smooth, autonomous, divergence-free velocity fields on $\mathbb{T}^3$ that generate fast dynamos. This resolves the Fast Dynamo Conjecture of Zeldovich and Sakharov, as recorded in Arnold's book of problems. We first construct a family of smooth, time-periodic fast dynamos via a stretch-fold-shear mechanism that applies to a large class of velocity fields. We then realize the associated period dynamics within a smooth autonomous flow using a global Poincaré section and a suitably prescribed return-time function. In both settings, the proof first isolates unstable distributional eigenmodes for the ideal induction operator on an anisotropic Banach space of distributions and then shows that this spectral instability persists under the singular perturbation $\varepsilon Δ$. The resulting estimates are uniform as $\varepsilon\to0$ and stable under $C^k$ perturbations of the velocity field, yielding an open set of smooth fast dynamo vector fields.

Halfspace Theorems for Anisotropic Minimal Surfaces and Perimeter Minimizers

Yang Yang

A classical result says a minimal surface in ordinary Euclidean geometry lying entirely in a halfspace must be a flat plane. This paper extends that halfspace theorem to anisotropic settings, where 'minimal' means minimizing a direction-dependent energy rather than plain area. The key innovation replaces the standard catenoid construction with a new elliptic-logarithm comparison surface, handling both smooth minimal surfaces in 3D and perimeter minimizers in higher dimensions.

math.AP
Original abstract

We prove two anisotropic halfspace theorems for uniformly elliptic parametric integrands. In $\mathbb{R}^3$, every connected smooth properly embedded boundaryless anisotropic minimal surface for an even integrand is a plane if it lies in a halfspace. The proof replaces the catenoid in the Hoffman--Meeks argument by a strict exterior graph modeled on an elliptic logarithm. In arbitrary dimension and without assuming evenness, every global anisotropic-perimeter minimizer whose nonempty boundary lies in a halfspace is itself a halfspace. The proof combines wall contact with a plane-peeling argument that turns any residual phase defect into a minimizer for the reversed integrand and excludes it by a second blow-down.

A counterexample to McKean's conjecture for the Landau-Coulomb equation

Jonathan Junné, Raphael Winter, Havva Yoldaş

McKean's conjecture predicted that entropy dissipation—a measure of how quickly a gas approaches equilibrium—never increases along solutions of the Landau-Coulomb equation, a model for plasma collisions. This paper disproves it with an explicit counterexample: a near-equilibrium state whose entropy dissipation temporarily rises. The finding challenges long-held intuitions about irreversibility and the approach to equilibrium in physically important plasma models.

math.AP
Original abstract

We disprove McKean's conjecture, which asserts that the entropy dissipation is monotone nonincreasing along solutions, or equivalently that the entropy is convex in time, for the spatially homogeneous Landau-Coulomb equation. We provide an explicit counterexample which consists of a Maxwellian equilibrium under radially symmetric, smooth perturbation on an annulus of scale $R\gg 1$. Developing the derivative of the entropy dissipation in powers of $R$ reveals that the leading order terms can be positive for a suitably chosen perturbation. Remarkably, the counterexamples are close to equilibrium in relative entropy and standard Sobolev norms. Since the Landau equation arises from the Boltzmann equation in the grazing collisions limit, our counterexample also shows that McKean's conjecture is not true in general for the Boltzmann equation.

Maximizing the fundamental Laplace--Neumann eigenvalue on quadrilaterals

Ryoki Endo, Braxton Osting

Among all convex quadrilaterals with equal area, which shape makes the first nonzero Neumann eigenvalue—the lowest vibration frequency with 'free' boundary conditions—as large as possible? This paper proves the answer is uniquely the square, tackling the quadrilateral case of a classical open problem analogous to the Pólya–Szegő conjecture. The proof combines a clever variational lower bound using the square's known eigenfunctions with careful geometric estimates across all quadrilateral shapes.

math.AP math.SP
Original abstract

We prove that among convex planar quadrilaterals of equal area, the square uniquely maximizes the first nonzero Laplace--Neumann eigenvalue. This is the quadrilateral case of the Neumann analogue of the long-standing Pólya--Szegő conjecture, which asserts that the regular $n$-gon minimizes the first Laplace--Dirichlet eigenvalue among $n$-gons of equal area. The proof uses a Rayleigh--Ritz lower bound, obtained from the span of the first five nonconstant Neumann modes of the square, on a smooth auxiliary functional whose minimization implies the original maximization. Local maximality at the square follows from a symmetry-reduced Hessian, computed in closed form, together with a certified second-order difference-quotient test that establishes the local inequality on an explicit ball around the square. Global maximality is then established by a certified box covering of the parameter space, certifying on each box a direct upper bound on the first Rayleigh--Ritz eigenvalue. Both certifications operate entirely on integrals over a fixed reference square and avoid a posteriori finite-element bounds on the perturbed quadrilateral.

Sharp convergence rates for the vanishing discount problem with hyperbolic Aubry sets

Panrui Ni

When a Hamilton–Jacobi PDE depends on a small 'discount' parameter λ→0, its solution converges to a canonical limit—but how fast? For Tonelli Hamiltonians (a broad class arising in classical mechanics) whose Aubry set—the collection of trajectories governing long-run behavior—consists of hyperbolic equilibria or periodic orbits, this paper establishes sharp convergence rates: the error is bounded below by Cλ and above by Cλ|log λ|, with the logarithmic correction being tight.

math.AP math.DS
Original abstract

Let $H\in C^2(T^*M)$ be a Tonelli Hamiltonian on a closed connected manifold and let $u_λ$ solve \[ λu_λ+H(x,Du_λ)=c(H)\qquad\text{in }M. \] We study the convergence rate of $u_λ$ to the selected critical solution $u_0$. Assume that the lifted Aubry set is a finite union $\widetilde{A}=Γ_1\sqcup\cdots\sqcupΓ_N$, where each $Γ_i$ is either a hyperbolic equilibrium or a periodic orbit hyperbolic in the critical energy level. We prove \[ -Cλ\le u_λ-u_0\le Cλ|\logλ|. \] Let $μ_i$ be the projected Mather measure associated with $Γ_i$. If \[ \int_M u_0\,\mathrm dμ_i=0\qquad \text{for every }i, \] then \[ \|u_λ-u_0\|_\infty\le Cλ. \] In particular, if the lifted Aubry set consists of a single hyperbolic equilibrium or a single hyperbolic periodic orbit, the convergence rate is $O(λ)$. We give examples showing that both convergence rates $O(λ)$ and $O(λ|\logλ|)$ are optimal. Without hyperbolicity, finite-order degenerate examples give lower bounds of order $λ^{1/(2r-1)}$ with $r\ge2$. We also construct infinite-order degenerate examples with arbitrarily slow convergence. Taken together, these results provide, to our knowledge, the first systematic quantitative theory for the vanishing discount problem in the Tonelli setting.

Interior Curvature Estimates for the Graphical Scalar Curvature Equation in All Dimensions

Guohuan Qiu, Jin Yan

The graphical scalar curvature equation describes hypersurfaces in Euclidean space with prescribed scalar curvature (the trace of the Ricci curvature tensor). The authors resolve a longstanding open problem by establishing interior C² estimates—bounds on second derivatives—for admissible solutions in all dimensions n≥3, using Jacobi inequalities combined with a two-surface maximum principle. Such estimates are foundational for understanding existence and regularity of solutions.

math.AP math.DG
Original abstract

We resolve the long-standing problem of establishing interior \(C^2\) estimates for admissible solutions of the graphical scalar curvature equation in every dimension \(n\ge 3\). More precisely, we prove interior curvature estimates for admissible solutions to the constant graphical scalar curvature equation. The proof combines Jacobi inequalities with a two-surface maximum principle and a two-surface Pogorelov estimate.

Principal eigenvalues of nonlocal operators with advection: sharp scaling limits and spectral phase transitions

Hoang-Hung Vo

Principal eigenvalues measure the threshold for stability in dispersal models; here the operators are nonlocal (averaging over a region rather than using derivatives) and include a drift term. Without drift, classical variational tools apply, but drift breaks this structure. The paper establishes sharp scaling laws and discovers spectral phase transitions — abrupt qualitative changes in eigenvalue behavior — as the nonlocal operator approaches its local (PDE) limit, resolving key open questions in spatial ecology models.

math.AP
Original abstract

We consider generalized principal eigenvalues of one-dimensional nonlocal dispersal operators with a drift of fixed sign. A principal difficulty in this non-self-adjoint setting is the absence of a variational characterization of the principal value. In the symmetric drift-free problem, the critical nonlocal-to-local limit can be treated through a quadratic variational structure and Sobolev-seminorm approximation as Berestycki-Coville-Vo \cite{BCV}. The drift destroys this structure and, on a bounded interval, introduces at the same time a one-sided inflow--outflow boundary geometry. Replacing the missing variational argument by estimates which remain stable under singular rescaling is therefore a central technical issue. Our approach is direct : we work with a single generalized principal value and prove the maximum principle, simplicity in the positive cone and the scaling limits from the equation itself, without passing through auxiliary generalized principal eigenvalues analogous to $λ_1'$ and $λ_1''$ in Berestycki--Rossi \cite{BR}. The replacement mechanisms are directional Harnack inequalities and coefficient barriers, direct--adjoint identities, logarithmic Collatz--Wielandt transforms, Fourier coercivity of zero extensions and scale-dependent localization. At the critical diffusive scale, Fourier compactness and an exact energy--transport identity recover the missing inflow Dirichlet condition and identify the local Dirichlet limit without a Rayleigh quotient. For variable coefficients we determine the complete small-range phase diagram on bounded intervals and on the line, and we obtain sharp large-range three-term asymptotics by a rank-one reduction and a corner Laplace analysis in the advective travel-time variable. In the homogeneous whole-line problem the critical correction is of order $σ^2$.

Time-asymptotic stability of generic Riemann solutions for the system of heat-conductive ideal gas without viscosity

Yilin Guo, Lin-An Li, Jiahong Wu et al.

Can solutions to compressible gas equations without viscosity still stabilize over time into predictable wave patterns? This paper proves that for a heat-conducting ideal gas with no viscosity, solutions starting near a combination of a shock, contact discontinuity, and rarefaction wave converge uniformly to a composite wave as time goes to infinity, demonstrating long-term stability despite the absence of viscous smoothing.

math.AP
Original abstract

This paper is concerned with the time-asymptotic stability of the generic Riemann solution for the one-dimensional system of heat-conductive ideal gas without viscosity, where the generic Riemann solution consists of a shock, a contact discontinuity, and a rarefaction wave. We prove that, as time tends to infinity, the solution of the non-viscous and heat-conductive ideal gas system converges uniformly to a composite wave composed of rarefaction wave, viscous contact wave, and viscous shock wave with a time-dependent shift. Motivated by the recent work of Kang-Vasseur-Wang [Arch. Ration. Mech. Anal. 249: 42 (2025)], we overcome the difficulties arising from the concurrence of shock and rarefaction waves for the partially dissipative hyperbolic-parabolic system with dissipation acting only on a single variable. More notably, the absence of velocity dissipation gives rise to new and intrinsic difficulties when handling the terms associated with the density and velocity. To resolve this, we exploit the precise structure of the governing equations and the additional properties of shock waves. Furthermore, we utilize the wave structure of the system without viscosity and perform separate space-time estimates for the density and velocity.

Quantum Landau Damping with Coulomb Repulsion in the Whole Space

Dongfen Bian, Emmanuel Grenier, Laurent Laflèche et al.

Landau damping describes how plasma oscillations decay over time even without dissipation; this paper establishes this phenomenon for the quantum (Hartree) equation with Coulomb repulsion, proving the electric field decays in time uniformly as Planck's constant varies. Crucially, it also shows the quantum density converges to its classical counterpart as ℏ→0, bridging quantum and classical plasma physics.

math.AP
Original abstract

In this paper, we study the large time behavior of solutions to the linearized Hartree equation near a stable equilibrium which is homogeneous in space and investigate the quantum Landau damping. This allows us to get time decay of the electric field, uniformly in the Planck constant, as well as the uniform-in-time convergence of the quantum density towards the classical density as the Planck constant converges to 0.

The entropy production is not always monotone for hard spheres or for Maxwell molecules

Luis Silvestre

The Boltzmann equation governs gas dynamics, and entropy production measures how quickly a gas moves toward equilibrium—McKean conjectured in 1966 that this rate must itself decrease monotonically over time. This paper disproves that conjecture, constructing explicit counterexamples—radially symmetric mixtures of Maxwellian distributions—for both hard spheres and Maxwell molecules. The result overturns a 60-year-old intuition about the approach to thermal equilibrium in kinetic theory.

math.AP math-ph
Original abstract

In 1966, McKean asked whether the entropy production of the Boltzmann equation must be monotone decreasing in time. We show that this is not the case even in the space-homogeneous setting for the Boltzmann collision operator with a constant angular cross section and the kinetic parameter $γ\in [0,1]$. This recovers the classical case of hard spheres and the simplest case of Maxwell molecules. Our examples are radially symmetric mixtures of Maxwellians.

Failure of Interior Hölder and Gradient Estimates for the Subcritical Special Lagrangian Equation

Zhenyu Fan, Caiyan Li, Zhehui Wang

The special Lagrangian equation governs certain minimal surfaces in complex geometry, and analysts hoped solutions would automatically be somewhat smooth (Hölder continuous) inside their domain. This paper kills that hope: explicit analytic solutions show that in dimensions 3 and higher, subcritical-phase solutions can oscillate arbitrarily badly with no shared modulus of continuity, meaning no interior regularity estimates of this type can hold.

math.AP
Original abstract

For every $n\ge3$, we show, by a family of explicit entire real-analytic solutions, that, for every subcritical phase and every $0<α\le1$, oscillation-based interior $C^α$ estimates fail for the special Lagrangian equation in $\mathbb{R}^n$. In fact, this family has no common interior modulus of continuity.

Asymptotic Expansions of the Fundamental Solution for a Mixed Local-Nonlocal Operator

Congming Li, Ling Wang, Jiongduo Xie

The mixed local-nonlocal operator −Δ+(−Δ)^s blends ordinary diffusion (the Laplacian) with long-range fractional diffusion, making it hard to analyze near the origin or at infinity. This paper derives sharp asymptotic expansions of the operator's fundamental solution — the basic 'point-source' response — capturing the transition between the two diffusion regimes, including delicate logarithmic corrections. These expansions are then applied to establish distributional identities and precise decay estimates relevant to PDE theory.

math.AP
Original abstract

In this paper, we study the fundamental solution of the mixed local-nonlocal operator $ -Δ+(-Δ)^s,$ $ 0<s<1. $ We derive precise asymptotic expansions of the fundamental solution and its gradient, up to the first nontrivial correction term, both near the origin and at infinity. The expansions describe the transition between the local and nonlocal diffusion scales and include the critical regimes in which logarithmic terms occur. As applications of these asymptotics, we establish a distributional Bôcher-type theorem for nonnegative supersolutions with an isolated singularity. We further obtain quantitative positive and antisymmetric maximum principles on punctured balls.

Delaunay-type interface in a screened model of diblock copolymer melts

Guy Foghem, Mouhamed Moustapha Fall

Diblock copolymers—chains of two chemically distinct polymers bonded together—self-organize into periodic patterns; can we prove infinitely many such patterns exist mathematically? This paper establishes existence of infinitely many smooth, periodic 3D domain patterns of Delaunay type (surfaces of revolution with constant mean curvature) as stationary sets of a screened version of the Ohta–Kawasaki energy functional. This extends the mathematical theory of phase-separation patterns in soft matter to a broader family of equilibrium shapes.

math.AP
Original abstract

A diblock copolymer is a soft-matter composed of two chemically distinct block of repeating monomers covalently bonded together at an end-to-end junction to form a single polymer chain. In this paper, we establish the existence of infinitely many smooth periodic unbounded domain patterns of Delaunay-type in $\mathbb{R}^3$ that optimize the energy distribution in diblock copolymer melts. We emphasize that pattern domains at the equilibrium correspond to stationary sets of the screened Ohta--Kawasaki free energy functional \begin{align*} \mathcal{P}_γ(Ω) := |\partialΩ| + γ\int_Ω\int_Ω G_κ(|x-y|) \,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y, \end{align*} where $γ>0$, $κ>0$ and $G_κ(r)=\frac{1}{r} e^{-κr}$ is the repulisive Yukawa potential. Equivalently, these equilibria satisfy the corresponding Euler--Lagrange equation \begin{align*} \mathcal{H}_Ω(x):= H_{\partialΩ}(x) + γ\int_Ω G_κ(|x-y|) \mathrm{d}y = \textrm{Const} \quad \text{on } \partialΩ, \end{align*} where $H_{\partialΩ}$ denotes the mean curvature of the surface $\partialΩ$. By analyzing the linearization of $Ω\mapsto \mathcal{H}_Ω$ around flat cylinders and applying the Crandall--Rabinowitz bifurcation theorem, for any $κ> 0$ and sufficiently small $γ> 0$, we prove the existence of non-trivial, $2π$-periodic Delaunay-type equilibrium cylinder interfaces with shapes close to a Delaunay unduloid surface of constant mean curvature.

Global regularity for 2D gravity water waves with two retreating point vortices

Lei Su, Qingtang Su, Siwei Wang

Water wave equations are notoriously difficult to solve globally because nonlinear effects can cause solutions to break down in finite time. This paper proves that 2D gravity water waves coupled to a pair of opposite-strength point vortices remain well-behaved for all time, provided the vortex pair starts deep below the surface and moves away from it. The key insight is that retreating vortices produce velocity that decays fast enough at the free surface to prevent the formation of singularities.

math.AP
Original abstract

We prove global well-posedness for the two-dimensional infinite-depth gravity water wave system coupled to a pair of point vortices of opposite strengths. The vortex dipole is initially placed deep below the free surface and oriented so that its leading motion is away from the interface. We show that the vortices retreat almost linearly, and that the velocity induced on the free boundary is therefore integrable in time. Combining this decay with Wu's cubic formulation, together with a transition-of-derivatives and localization argument, we obtain global bounds for small localized perturbations of the flat surface.

The $α$-Limit Problem: Convergence of a Linear Degenerate Interface Transmission Problem

Toai Luong, Tadele Mengesha, Kerrek Stinson et al.

When you want to solve equations on complex domains with interfaces, a common trick is to 'smear out' the interface and solve a smoother problem instead. This paper analyzes what happens as that smearing vanishes (α→0) in a new two-parameter method, proving the regularized solutions converge to the correct interface problem. The result validates the method rigorously and characterizes the unusual energy structure that emerges at the limit.

math.AP math.NA
Original abstract

We study the singular limit of a family of linear degenerate interface transmission problems arising from a regularization procedure in the newly proposed Two-Parameter Diffuse Domain Method (DDM2p). For $α>0$, the regularized problem admits a strictly convex variational formulation on $H^{1}(Ω)$. In the limit $α\to0$, the problem degenerates to a weakly coupled interface system with a nonstandard energy structure. To characterize the limit, we introduce a closed Hilbert subspace $\mathcal{H}\subset H^{1}(Ω)$, defined through an auxiliary Helmholtz problem on an annular subdomain $Ω_2\subset Ω$, and identify the limiting energy functional $\mathcal{E}_{0}$ on $\mathcal{H}$. We prove that the regularized energies $\mathcal{E}_α$ $Γ$-converge to $\mathcal{E}_{0}$ in the strong $L^{2}(Ω)$ topology, using the standard framework. Consequently, minimizers of $\mathcal{E}_α$ converge to the unique minimizer of $\mathcal{E}_{0}$, which is shown to be equivalent to the solution of the limiting interface problem. We further prove strong convergence $u_α\to u_{0}$ in $H^{1}(Ω)$ and establish an $O(α)$ convergence rate. Numerical experiments in one spatial dimension confirm the predicted first-order convergence rate and suggest that this rate is sharp.

Obstruction to quasi-invariance of Gaussian measures under transport flows on Riemannian Manifolds

Anne-Sophie de Suzzoni, Mickaël Latocca

A Gaussian measure is 'quasi-invariant' under a flow if the transformed measure and the original share the same sets of measure zero; 'invariance' is the stronger condition that the measure is literally unchanged. This paper proves that for natural Gaussian fields on Riemannian manifolds, quasi-invariance under divergence-free transport flows forces full invariance, which happens only when the flow preserves the geometry (i.e., the vector field is Killing). The result sharply limits which flows can preserve these measures in any useful sense.

math.AP math.PR
Original abstract

We prove an obstruction to quasi-invariance of Gaussian fields under divergence free transport flows on Riemannian manifolds. For the centered Gaussian field with covariance $(1-Δ_g)^{-α}$, $α>\frac{d}{2}$, quasi-invariance under the transport flow is equivalent to invariance. This happens precisely when the flow acts by isometries, or equivalently when the underlying vector field is Killing. The proof leverages the Feldman--Hájek theorem and utilizes localized pseudo-differential computations. On $\mathbb R^d$ this leaves only rigid motions, while on flat tori this leaves only translations.

Gaussian upper bounds for averaged heat semigroups on graphs

Christian Rose

Heat semigroups on graphs describe how heat (or random walks) spreads over a network, and Gaussian upper bounds quantify how quickly this spreading decays with distance. Existing characterizations of these bounds on graphs with unbounded geometry carry errors that grow with vertex degree; this paper introduces a new space-time averaged framework that eliminates these errors using large-scale Faber-Krahn inequalities (which relate geometry to spectral gaps). The key technical tool is an integrated version of Davies' method, yielding robust bounds independent of local degree irregularities.

math.AP math.PR
Original abstract

Characterizations of pointwise Gaussian upper bounds on graphs with possibly unbounded geometry in terms of localized functional inequalities contain errors depending on the vertex degree. We introduce a new space-time averaged form of the heat semigroup in terms of time-averaged $\ell^{q/(q-1)}-\ell^q$-estimates and obtain Gaussian upper bounds from large-scale Faber-Krahn inequalities which avoid such errors. A main analytic ingredient is an integrated version of Davies' method which yields off-diagonal estimates for this averaged quantity, and which tends to pointwise Gaussian bounds as time tends to infinity. Conversely, on large scales volume doubling and Gaussian bounds on this averaged heat semigroup norms imply relative Faber-Krahn inequalities for subsets of prescribed relative measure. Our proof is based on a lower bound for Dirichlet eigenvalues in terms of these averages. The Faber-Krahn dimension is scale-dependent, but converges to the doubling dimension for increasing radii. This gives a characterization of asymptotic Gaussian heat kernel behavior in terms of Faber-Krahn inequalities.

Ideal MHD below the classical well-posedness threshold

Matteo Giardi

Magnetohydrodynamics (MHD) describes electrically conducting fluids like plasma. Classical theory requires the initial velocity field to be quite smooth for solutions to exist uniquely. This paper proves well-posedness for ideal incompressible MHD at lower smoothness thresholds than previously possible, exploiting a hidden algebraic 'Alfvén null-structure' in the equations. This matters because understanding solutions near the regularity threshold is crucial for both mathematical theory and physical modeling of phenomena like solar wind.

math.AP
Original abstract

We establish local existence and uniqueness of solutions for the ideal incompressible magnetohydrodynamics system posed on $[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^n$, $n\ge2$, with a nonzero constant initial magnetic field $\mathbf{B}_0$ and arbitrary divergence-free velocity data $v_0\in H^s$, in the range $(n+1)/2<s\le n/2+1$. The proof uses a Lagrangian wave--Hodge reformulation and exploits an Alfvén null--structure hidden in the pressure forcing. In particular, the constructed Eulerian solutions are induced by a bi-Lipschitz measure-preserving flow map. Zhang first identified this null-structure in \cite{Zhang2024}; the present work provides a self-contained bridge from that Lagrangian theory to the Eulerian Cauchy problem.

The Vlasov-Maxwell point charge system : study of the spherically symmetric case

Emile Breton

The Vlasov-Maxwell system models a plasma of charged particles interacting with electromagnetic fields; adding a fixed point charge creates singularities that make analysis hard. Under spherical symmetry, the problem reduces to a relativistic version of the classical Kepler (two-body) problem, letting the authors introduce action-angle variables—coordinates that simplify periodic motion. Using this framework, they prove that small, smooth initial data lead to globally defined classical solutions, avoiding finite-time blowup.

math.AP
Original abstract

We consider the Vlasov-Maxwell system with a repulsive point charge. Under a spherical symmetry condition, this reduces to the relativistic Vlasov-Poisson system with a point charge. For this system, the linear characteristics solve the equations of motion for the relativistic Kepler problem. We define action-angle variables for the linearized equation, and then study the nonlinear system in these coordinates. Within this framework, we prove global existence of classical solutions for small initial data and establish their modified scattering behavior. The proof makes use of the ideas developed in the non-relativistic case.

Sharp Mixed Spectral Barron Regularity of Coulombic Many-Electron Wave Functions

Pingbing Ming, Hao Yu

Electron wavefunctions in quantum chemistry have cusps where electrons meet nuclei, making their regularity subtle. This paper establishes sharp regularity results in the Barron space (functions whose Fourier transform is absolutely integrable with specific weights), capturing coordinate-wise smoothness that isotropic measures miss. The results are optimal and matter for machine-learning-based PDE solvers, where Barron regularity controls the complexity of neural network approximations.

math.AP cs.LG math.NA
Original abstract

We establish sharp mixed spectral Barron regularity for eigenfunctions of molecular Coulomb Hamiltonians. The mixed norm is a Fourier $L^1$ norm with one isotropic weight and coordinate-product weights, and therefore detects regularity invisible to the isotropic Barron scale. For a nonempty set $I$ of electron indices on which the wave function is antisymmetric, we derive an explicit admissible region for the isotropic order $s$ and the coordinate orders $α,β$. This region is optimal as a uniform statement over the class of clamped-nuclei Coulomb Hamiltonians. For fixed-spin components with two occupied spin blocks, it reduces to $s+α+β<1$; in the fully spin-polarized class it reduces to $s+α<1$. In particular, if $\mathcal I_σ$ denotes the family of occupied same-spin blocks determined by $σ$, then every fixed-spin spatial component $ψ_σ$ satisfies, for every $0\leqα<1$, \[ \left(\sum_{I\in\mathcal I_σ}\prod_{i\in I}\langleξ_i\rangle^α\right)\widehat{ψ_σ}\in L^1(\mathbb{R}^{3N}). \] For a fully spin-polarized state, $\mathcal I_σ=\{\{1,\ldots,N\}\}$.

Regularity and Rivière's ${GL}(m)$-Gauge Construction for Elliptic Systems with Antisymmetric Potentials in Arbitrary Dimensions

Carolin Bayer

Can solutions to certain nonlinear elliptic PDEs be continuous even when their coefficients are barely integrable? This paper extends Rivière's landmark gauge-theoretic method—which rewrites a PDE with antisymmetric potential into a more tractable divergence form—from the classical L² setting to refined Lorentz-space settings in dimensions three and above, proving continuity of solutions under near-optimal integrability assumptions. This sharpens regularity theory for geometric PDEs arising in harmonic map and liquid crystal problems.

math.AP math.DG
Original abstract

Let $1 \leq q \le 2$ and denote by $2 \leq q'$ its corresponding conjugate exponent. We prove the continuity of solutions $u \in W^{1,(\frac{n}{n-1},q')}(B^n, \mathbb{R}^m)$ to the critical elliptic system $-Δu = Ω\cdot \nabla u$ in dimension $n \ge 3$, where the potential $Ω\in L^{(n,q)}(B^n, \mathfrak{so}(m) \otimes \wedge^1)$ is antisymmetric. First, we construct $P \in W^{1,(n,q)}(B^n, \mathrm{SO}(m))$ such that the PDE can be rewritten as $-\operatorname{div}(P^{-1}du) = \ast dξ\cdot P^{-1}du$, which is nearly a Jacobian structure up to the rotation $P$. Second, we provide a Rivière's $\mathrm{GL}(m)$-Gauge in order to establish a "full" $(A,B)$-conservation law, i.e. $-\operatorname{div}(Adu)=d^\ast B \cdot du$. We show that the assumption on $Ω\in L^{(n,q)} (B^n, \mathfrak{so}(m) \otimes \wedge^1) $ for $q\leq 2$ is optimal.

Lp Brunn-Minkowski inequality for the eigenvalue of generalized Monge-Ampère equation

Yuxiao Yan, Feida Jiang

The Lp Brunn-Minkowski inequality generalizes classical convex geometry by asking how geometric quantities scale under Lp Minkowski addition, a continuous family of ways to combine convex bodies. This paper proves that the principal eigenvalue of a generalized Monge-Ampère equation — a key PDE in convex geometry — satisfies this inequality, extending the theory to an important new analytic setting.

math.AP
Original abstract

We investigate the eigenvalue of a generalized Monge-Ampère equation. We proved that the eigenvalue of the equation on the Lp Minkowksi addition of two convex bodies satisfies the Lp Brunn-Minkowski inequality.

Shuhei Kitano

The Aleksandrov–Bakelman–Pucci (ABP) estimate is a fundamental tool for bounding solutions to elliptic PDEs using the geometry of their graphs. This paper extends ABP estimates to the weighted 1-Laplacian—an operator where the usual smoothness assumptions break down—by replacing the classical concave envelope construction with a quasiconcave envelope, enabling maximum-principle-type control in this degenerate setting.
